| 183 | ✓ Built | **✓ Built 2026-08-31, same session as the spec, after Phase 182's FR-182-2; FR-183-7 live-measured 2026-09-02 against the real deployment and the exact reported series — not dev-reviewed.** `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, `linuxX64Test` 163/163. Deliberate deviation from FR-183-1's literal wording: the rate limiter is scoped to `TmdbClient` (`TmdbRateLimiter`, one token bucket, seeded 4 req/s / burst 10 / floor 0.5 / ceiling 20, AIMD-adjusted) rather than generic inside `OutboundHttp` — threading a host param through `OutboundHttp.withPermit` would touch every caller (Jellyfin/Seerr/Bazarr/*arr) for a TMDB-specific problem. `httpGet` now acquires a token before every request, honours `Retry-After`, backs off exponentially (1s base, 20s cap) with **full jitter** (`Random.nextLong`) otherwise, and throws `TmdbRateLimitExhaustedException` on exhaustion instead of returning the raw 429 for a caller to fail to deserialize. New `Scanner.episodeFanoutGate` (`scan_workers × 3`, clamped `[3,24]`, fixed at construction) bounds the two TMDB call sites inside `scanSeries`/`syncSeriesEpisodes`'s per-episode `async` blocks. Both functions now skip the TMDB details/credits fetch entirely when the previous scan already holds good data — deliberately conservative (never mistakes a genuinely-empty TMDB response for "not fetched yet"). **Found and fixed a real latent data-loss bug as a side effect**: `scanSeries`'s episode fields had NO fallback to the existing value at all before this — any transient TMDB failure on a re-scan silently blanked already-good title/overview/still/runtime/airDate; both functions now fall back to the existing episode on every one of those fields. **FR-183-5/FR-183-6 both built 2026-09-02.** FR-183-5's DB-level half: new `TmdbExhaustionTracker` (a `CoroutineContext.Element`, same shape as `WorkerId`) installed by `PipelineStepOps.pullTmdb` around the whole `rescanMetadata` call, marked at `httpGet`'s exact throw site regardless of how many `runCatching` layers downstream swallow the exception; a hit marks the item dirty via **Phase 181's own persistent dirty-set**, so the next `RunTarget.Library` cycle retries it — no new retry mechanism needed since 181 built one the same day. FR-183-6: new `TmdbClient.pacingStats()` (rate/ceiling/floor + a rolling 429-count over the last minute) rides the existing `GET /api/health` probe; the Activity page's Jobs & workers view polls it into a new "Outbound pacing" card. **FR-183-7 measured 2026-09-02** against TMDB id 2777 ("Oscar and the Beetles," 505 episodes — the exact reported series): `tmdb_pacing` recorded zero 429s across every test this session, rate held at its 20/s ceiling — real evidence for the core fix. Full before/after wasn't fillable (the "before" code no longer exists to re-measure). **New finding**: `POST /api/media/{id}/sync` against this series reliably 503'd with `ProcessGate saturated (interactive, 1500ms)` within ~2s, reproduced from a cold gate — `syncSeriesEpisodes`'s per-file `filesToProbe.map { async { FfprobeRunner.probe(file) } }` is completely unbounded (unlike the TMDB fetch just below it, which FR-183-3 did bound), and because this manual-sync path carries no `GateClass` tag it defaults INTERACTIVE (Phase 182's fail-safe) — 505 concurrent ffprobe calls self-saturate the same 4-permit interactive-reserved lane real Ravilo requests depend on. Outside both 182's and 183's own FRs; filed as a follow-up candidate, not fixed inline. See the spec's own build note for the full account. **Pace outbound requests by rate, not just by concurrency** — reported 2026-08-31: *"when doing a scan we almost everytime get rate-limited… even though we have amount of 'workers', when we are working with series it just goes ahead and starts it's own concurrency"*. The user's diagnosis is correct and the supplied log proves it: **18 TMDB 429s all timestamped `19:05:03`, all carrying the same `[W#3]` worker id**, spanning attempts 1/5 through 5/5. `WorkerId` is a `CoroutineContext` element installed once per worker (`MediaRoutes.kt:2252`) and **inherited by every child coroutine**, so one worker id on 18 concurrent requests is direct proof of unbounded fan-out below the worker level. Findings: (1) `Scanner.scanSeries` (`:504-585`) and `syncSeriesEpisodes` (`:838-903`) both do `filesToProbe.map { async { … } }.awaitAll()` with **no ceiling** — a 300-episode series dispatches 300 coroutines from one of four workers; the code comment justifying this (`:496-503`) is sound for ffprobe (`ProcessGate` bounds a real resource) and does not hold for TMDB. (2) Per episode that is **up to 5-6 requests** — `getEpisodeDetailsLocalized` (`TmdbClient.kt:761-779`) walks the language list plus a regional retry plus an untagged fallback, and `fetchEpisodeCredits` (`Scanner.kt:1366`) is **unconditional**, re-fetched on every scan; ~1 500-2 000 requests for the reported series alone. (3) `httpGet`'s 429 branch (`:350-363`) is a **flat 3 s with no jitter, no backoff and no `Retry-After`** — which is exactly why 18 lines share one second. (4) **The only throttle in the system limits concurrency, and concurrency is not rate**: 64 permits × ~100 ms round-trip ≈ **640 req/s**, rising as latency falls. There is no token bucket or per-host pacing anywhere. `pipelineStepConcurrency`'s `sync_imdb_ratings → coerceIn(1,2)` special case (`PipelineStepPool.kt:150-159`) shows the team already met this problem and solved it once, narrowly. (5) **Being rate-limited silently loses metadata** — after 5 attempts `httpGet` *returns* the 429 response, callers `.body<T>()` it inside `runCatching`, get null, and write the episode with null title/overview/still/runtime and no cast, stamped as freshly scanned; nothing distinguishes "TMDB has no data" from "429'd five times", and Phase 181's freshness filter then treats the gap as settled. (6) Retries re-enter the same 64-permit queue from the back, which is the queue Phase 182 §2.5 shows starving Ravilo — **the two reported incidents are one incident**. Plan: FR-183-1 per-host token bucket inside `OutboundHttp` (TMDB publishes **no** current numeric limit — the 40/10 s figure was withdrawn in 2019 — so the default is a conservative configurable constant, never a guessed number asserted as fact); FR-183-2 `Retry-After` + exponential backoff + **full jitter**, with 429s feeding back into the bucket AIMD-style so the real ceiling is found empirically; FR-183-3 bounded per-item fan-out **derived from `scan_workers`** so the operator's "4" means something end to end; FR-183-4 stop re-fetching credits/details we already hold — **the single largest lever**, taking a re-scan of the reported series from ~1 500-2 000 requests to ~0; FR-183-5 an exhausted 429 is a typed outcome that does *not* stamp the item as checked; FR-183-6 rate/429s visible on Activity while a run is in flight; FR-183-7 a before/after measurement table where wall-clock is allowed to regress and "episodes written with null title/overview" is not. Do not ship without Phase 182's FR-182-2 — this phase makes `TmdbClient`'s two unsynchronised caches much hotter. Design-authored 2026-08-31 from a source read; not yet dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-183-outbound-request-pacing.md) |

| 211 | ✓ Built | **`PlaystateCache` never fetched an episode id, so every series lost its own progress and next-up** — spec'd and built 2026-09-14, from a live report on the stue TV hours after Phase 205 deployed: "All progress is gone on every series. The pre-205 code took whatever id list the caller passed and fetched exactly those, so it had no such gap; the regression is specific to the cache's own background fetch never being told to include episode ids. Fixed: the id list now flattens each item's own id with every one of its episodes' ids (`item.episodes.mapNotNull { it.jellyfinId }`), pulled into a new internal `idsToRefresh()` so it's unit-testable without a live/faked Jellyfin call — no change to `fetchPlaystate`/`getUserDataBulk` (both already take an arbitrary id list and already chunk it) or to `DetailService.getPlaystate`/`hydrateRelated` (both were already correct reads of whatever the map held). **Untested by the existing suite because of it**: `HomeFeedServiceReadPathTest`'s fixtures all use `episodes = emptyList()`, so no prior test exercised a series with episodes through this path. **Traded-off cost, accepted**: materially grows the per-cycle outbound id count for large series (Three and a Half Uncles: 12 seasons × 24 episodes) — absorbed by the existing `HYDRATE_CHUNK` chunking and `hydrateGate` concurrency cap, no separate pacing added; flagged as exactly the kind of live A/B FR-205-7 (deliberately not built in 205) would catch if it reproduces Phase 183's TMDB-fan-out shape against Jellyfin. `compileKotlinLinuxX64` clean, full `linuxX64Test` suite green including 3 new `PlaystateCacheTest` cases pinning the regression. | 213 | ✓ Built | **`prewarm_subtitles` moves to a shared job-queue pool, and stops out-running playback** — design-authored 2026-09-15 from a live incident, revised the same day to merge three independently-sized lanes into one configurable pool, implemented 2026-09-15, not dev-reviewed, not deployed/live-verified. A viewer 30 minutes into *The Patriarch* hit a stall every ~10 s from disk starvation caused by **10 concurrent Jellyfin ffmpeg subtitle extractions**, all requested by `prewarm_subtitles`'s pipeline-step fan-out — see the spec for the full incident/root-cause writeup. Built: `MediaJobQueue` now runs three named FIFO queues (`media`/`segments`/`subtitles`) off **one shared worker pool** (`behavior.job_workers`, 1–3, default 2, replacing `behavior.segment_workers`), with the rule that a queue may never have more than one worker drawing from it at a time — which is what preserves the media queue's existing same-file-remux safety without a dedicated dispatcher, and is why `bulk_reorder` still blocks other media-queue work but never segments/subtitles. `PipelineStepOps.prewarmSubtitles` now re-checks playback and cancellation **between every stream** and treats a Jellyfin timeout (`JellyfinClient.WarmResult.TimedOut`) as "abandon the rest of this item's list," not "try the next index" — the exact defect that built the incident's backlog. A playback-deferred job re-queues in place (same row, same FIFO position); a timed-out one retries with 5/10/15-minute backoff, capped at 3 attempts, then fails outright. The `prewarm_subtitles` pipeline step is now enqueue-only (Phase 164's `detect_segments` pattern) and always defers regardless of trigger kind; `launchScanRun`'s `deferEligible` no longer requires `triggerKind == "scheduled"` (FR-213-6), fixing the same bug for every other deferrable step. `GET /api/health` gained a `job_queues` block (FR-213-7). `compileKotlinLinuxX64`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`linuxX64Test` all clean. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-213-prewarm-subtitles-to-serialized-job-lane.md) |

| 215 | ✓ Built | **A memory budget calculator: "here is the RAM I'll give it, tell me what to change"** — design-authored 2026-09-15 to the owner's own brief, implemented 2026-09-15, not dev-reviewed, not deployed/live-verified (see the spec's §6: resolves open questions 1/2/5 during implementation — a `max(8GB, 10% of host RAM)` reserve stated as an assumption not a measurement, one combined budget figure with jellystructure getting a fixed 4GB slice, and a Settings → Advanced card rather than a dedicated page; the Jellyfin `mem_limit` and `/dev/shm` cap findings are unconditional by necessity since jellystructure can't read Jellyfin's compose file or host tmpfs size from its own container, while the `vm.swappiness` finding IS real silence-when-correct since that sysctl is host-wide and genuinely live-readable). Phase 212 is a differ (live value vs recommendation); this is a **calculator** — one number in, concrete copy-pasteable changes out across three places that must agree: Jellyfin's own settings, both `docker-compose.yml` files, and host sysctls. Motivated by measurements showing this host's memory is enormous and **entirely ungoverned**: **Jellyfin runs with no memory limit at all** (`HostConfig.Memory = 0`), its `/transcode` is a bind mount of the **host's** `/dev/shm` at the kernel default **63 GB** (so the container's own `shm_size: 64 MB` does not bound it), `EnableSegmentDeletion` is `false` with `SegmentKeepSeconds: 720` so segments accumulate in that RAM disk for a whole session, and the kernel is already swapping **12 GB** out at `vm.swappiness = 60` while holding 102 GB of page cache. Nothing has fallen over yet, but nothing is decided either, and the failure mode is a host OOM mid-transcode. Suggest-only, same posture as 212. Key rules: show the arithmetic (FR-215-1); **page cache is neither free memory nor spare memory** and must never be presented as capacity to allocate away (FR-215-2); a tmpfs cap is always paired with "Delete segments" because a size cap alone just converts an OOM into a failed transcode (FR-215-3); an unlimited container is always a finding, stated as *which* failure the operator is choosing (FR-215-4); and **refuse to emit anything** rather than emit a best-effort over-commit with a warning above it, since the copy-pasteable block always wins over the warning (FR-215-7). **FR-215-8** (added same day, from the owner asking what happens if another service later takes 60 GB) makes surviving a later neighbour a first-class requirement, and the answer splits by memory kind: **page cache is reclaimable so any prefetch degrades correctly with no work from us** — the hoped-for "skip it and clients still work" is already true there — but **tmpfs is not reclaimable and is the actual break path** (`/dev/shm` 63 GB cap feeding `/transcode`, plus a second unbounded 63 GB `/tmp` already holding 6.2 GB). A 60 GB neighbour alone is survivable; a 60 GB neighbour *plus* an uncapped transcode filling tmpfs is not. Also records that `jellyfin`, `immich_server`, `immich_postgres` and `umami-db` all run with **no memory limit** and that OOM scores are effectively tied (`jellyfin` 671 vs `jellystructure` 678, both `oom_score_adj = 0`), so the kernel currently picks its victim by RSS heuristic — the process serving a film is as likely as a batch job. Explicitly useful without the Phase 212 open-question-6 prefetch feature ever being built. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-215-memory-budget-calculator.md) |

Fix: one shared id per toolchain kind across all three Dockerfiles — safe, since the job already builds them sequentially on purpose (peak-memory reasons) and BuildKit's own locking makes concurrent local use safe too. Together: a phone could list a paired screen, but every play/pause/stop/seek command sent to it went nowhere. **A fourth gap found and left alone, per phase 245's own notes:** `/api/remote/devices/{id}`'s `now_playing.playing` field is dropped entirely (not sent as `false`) by the global `ContentNegotiation`'s `encodeDefaults=false` while paused — confirmed as the exact "second symptom" 245 predicted and deliberately deferred; the test asserts `toBeFalsy()`, not `toBe(false)`. CORS: `ravilo-screen`'s container gets a second network alias (`ravilo-screen-cast`, allow-listed) rather than a second image, so 247's proof that the bare `ravilo-screen` origin is refused stays untouched. `ravilo-cast`'s identical missing-`install(WebSockets)` gap is latent (never calls `connectEvents()`) and left alone. Chromecast is explicitly out of scope: CAF needs a real Cast device. Verified locally: 5+ clean runs against a real backend, the WebSockets revert-and-confirm, and 247's specs re-run alongside with no regression. **Two real CI runs needed after that**, neither about casting itself: the first failed on 249's CSP finding (below); the second on an actual gap in this spec's own test — it never triggered its own library scan at all (relied on local manual setup that never made it into the committed spec), so `/api/tv/search` found nothing on a truly fresh CI backend. Fixed: the spec now triggers `POST /api/scan` and polls `/api/scan/status` itself, with a 90s allowance for a shared, busy runner. | 244 | ✓ Built | **Catch the Jellyfin configuration that leaves this server open, and say how to close it** — written 2026-09-18 from the upgrade audit, **dev-reviewed 2026-09-19** (details at the end of this row), not built. Owner: the security issues we found are my own misconfiguration, so jellystructure should catch them and guide the fix. **Anyone on the internet can currently restart the household's Jellyfin with no credential** — not a Jellyfin bug but three correct behaviours meeting: `RestartApplication` allows unauthenticated in-network callers by policy, Jellyfin classifies by source address, and an empty `KnownProxies` behind Caddy makes every internet request in-network. Verified live: `GET /System/Endpoint` with `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.9` returns `IsInNetwork: true`. Reaches CVE-2025-32012's impact without its spoofing. Adds a security section to phase 212's advisor: a read-only capability probe (RFC 5737 range, never a real third-party address), a finding stating the consequence rather than the setting, a **Re-check** action so a fix is verified in place rather than guessed at, and an honest second finding about Jellyfin's unauthenticated media routes that offers no fix because none exists — explicitly warning that proxy-level auth would break Ravilo playback. Silence when already correct; no lifecycle probes ever (FR-240-6). Sibling: 242. **Dev-reviewed 2026-09-19 against `main` `dcb97f2c`**: the finding is real and the direction right, but **one measurement is missing that decides whether FR-244-1 works at all** — the household's `jellyfin_url` is `https://jellyfin.example.net`, i.e. jellystructure reaches Jellyfin *through Caddy*, which appends to `X-Forwarded-For`, so the probe may report `IsInNetwork: true` whether or not `KnownProxies` is set (the audit's two table rows were measured in different topologies, and the discriminating one never went through this path). Also: the advisor's 5-minute cache (`JellyfinAdvisorService.kt:27`) defeats FR-244-4's **Re-check**; `AdvisorFinding` has no severity and no action, which 242 needs too — extend it once; and **FR-244-2 gates on the wrong server** — `public_url` is *jellystructure's* address (`jelly.example.net`) while Jellyfin is exposed separately, so OQ2's `EnableRemoteAccess` is the right condition and OQ3 closes with it. **Built 2026-09-19** on top of 246. The review’s blocking item 1 is answered by not depending on the unmeasured case: FR-244-1 is **two signals** — an empty `KnownProxies` is decisive on its own (Jellyfin then ignores `X-Forwarded-For` outright, *observed* live 2026-09-19 as `IsInNetwork: true` identically with and without the RFC 5737 header), while a non-empty one hands the question to the probe and, if it still reports in-network, renders a distinct lower-severity **unconfirmed** finding naming a wrong value and a pending restart as the two candidates rather than claiming the hole is open or closed. Item 2: Re-check is its own cache-bypassing route (`/api/jellyfin/exposure-recheck`); `/health/full` calls the non-invalidating variant so a polled endpoint can’t clear an advisory cache. Item 3: `severity`/`action` came from 246. Item 4: gates on Jellyfin’s own `EnableRemoteAccess`, closing **OQ2 and OQ3**. Item 6: both the finding and the Re-check reply say a restart may be needed and to do it when nobody is watching, so OQ1’s answer doesn’t change the copy. FR-244-7 held — only `GET /System/Configuration/network` and `GET /System/Endpoint` are ever contacted. **Acceptance 2 outstanding by design**: `KnownProxies` is still empty on the household server and setting it is the operator’s to do. | [spec](specs/requirements/phase-244-jellyfin-exposure-advisor.md) |

## Ravilo phases (Android TV · Web · phone)

| Phase | Status | Focus | Spec |
|-------|--------|-------|------|
| R245 | ✓ Built | **Casting: the receiver, the sender, and the remote in your hand** — **implemented 2026-09-16**: shared `CastMessages.kt` (namespace, LOAD customData, receiver↔phone messages) + `castHandoff`/`castRedeem` on the client; a `CastSender` seam whose Android actual is the Cast SDK (`CastContext`, `RemoteMediaClient`, `MediaRouteButton`, `RaviloCastOptionsProvider` with the SDK notification, `MediaTransferReceiver` in the manifest; app id applied at runtime) and whose web actual is null; `CastConnectingBar`, `CastMiniBar` (never dismisses), `CastRemoteScreen` with the seven states and the SAME `TrackPicker` sheet (*Applies on {device}*), *Play on {device}* on both detail screens, the in-player hand-off via `LocalCastHandoff`, silent re-connect when finished/gone; a new Kotlin/JS `ravilo-cast` module (CAF via `dynamic`) that enrols with the hand-off code, negotiates its own ticket with probed capabilities, reports progress/stop, advances and auto-skips intros itself, and renders the ten screens; bundled by `syncCastReceiver` into `cast-receiver/` and packaged by the Dockerfile; 16 strings × en/da/fo. Compiles everywhere, UI tests green, **no real Chromecast tested**. — the viewer half of **218**, design-authored 2026-09-16, **dev-reviewed 2026-09-16** (no viewer-facing change; the reuse claim checks out — `shared`'s `js(IR)` target has shipped since R189 and `StreamTicket.hlsUrl` already carries its own `api_key=`, so the receiver is a Kotlin client needing no header plumbing — but its dependency on 218's enrolment is heavier than the cross-reference suggested, and open question 2 narrowed: a cast note *would* resolve, since the receiver is a device with its own history, so the real question is whether a TV-startup expectation belongs on a phone screen), built into the mockups the same day. **The receiver is its own Ravilo device** (FR-R245-13), a CAF web receiver rather than a screen — the one architectural fact that shapes nearly every frame: the TV keeps playing if the phone dies, it **advances and skips by itself** (FR-R245-14), and the remote **rebuilds from the receiver** on app start rather than from anything the phone remembered. **Re-connect is a requirement with two outcomes, one of which is silence** (FR-R245-5): finished or gone means no bar, no toast and no error, and the viewer casts again with one tap. The cast button is **server-pushed and absent, never greyed** (FR-R245-1), and the device picker is **the platform's own dialog**, which we do not redesign (FR-R245-2). Connecting is a bar, not a screen (FR-R245-3); casting from a detail screen hands the position over (FR-R245-4); the 64 px mini bar **never dismisses while a cast runs** (FR-R245-6); the full-screen remote is direction 2 · *Now playing* (FR-R245-7) with seven states (FR-R245-9), and leaving it never stops the cast (FR-R245-10). **Subtitles are one tap from the remote**, via *the same picker component the local player opens* — one component, two destinations — applied on the TV with one line saying so (FR-R245-8). The receiver's ten screens are **none of them interactive** (FR-R245-15), never talk about settings and never name a product, protocol, codec or status code (FR-R245-16), and stay **light enough for a 2013 stick** (FR-R245-17) — idle is the lit mark and one sentence, and *ended* is literally the idle view, because that is the decision. Reuses **R218**'s waiting moments, **R237**'s failure copy, **R180**/**R195**'s picker and **R222**'s slow-to-start note unchanged. Open questions: the stick's generation (see 218), whether the remote should carry R222's note for a cast, how a position disagreement resolves visually (a snap after a long drag will read as a bug), re-connect while a *different* item is playing, and what the mini bar shows during a receiver-side stall. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R245-cast-sender-receiver-and-remote.md) |
| R246 | ✓ Built | **Implemented 2026-09-16 (unit-tested, not on-device)** — `resolveTrackChoice` takes the track lists only and builds its own groups (the stale `currentAudioGroups`/`currentSubGroups` readers are deleted), the poll tick resolves against the lists it read, and a grown track set re-resolves once to fill an `Off`; 20 tests incl. the three observed transitions and the audio case. FR-R246-6's stue-TV check still owed. — **A remembered subtitle must actually reach the next episode** — spec'd 2026-09-16 from the live stue-TV sweep (F1, P1), not dev-reviewed. **R241's report reproduced three times on R241's own build** (S17E07 Dansk → S17E08 English; S08E04 opens Off; Dansk on S08E04 → S08E09 Off) and what gets selected is always exactly the source-default fallback tier. Code-traced cause: the poll loop (`PlayerScreen.kt:784+`) writes `audioTracks`/`subtitleTracks` and calls `resolveTrackSelection()` in the same tick with no suspension point, but the language groups it reads (`currentAudioGroups`/`currentSubGroups`, R196's `rememberUpdatedState`) are computed at **composition** — one composition behind, i.e. **empty** on the tick the tracks arrive (or the outgoing episode's, which `flatIndex < subtitleTracks.size` then discards). R241's `sameLanguage()` is correct and never reached; its test passes because it builds groups from the list it resolves. Ruled out with evidence: `persistChoice`'s key (every entry point passes `seriesId`, and the global tier is written too) and sidecars (though every `da` in the series is one — 77 `dan` embedded, 16 `da` external, zero crossover; the fixture keeps them). FRs: `resolveTrackChoice` builds its own groups from the lists it is handed and the `*Groups` params go away (FR-R246-1/2); a fixture from the real file shapes (FR-R246-3), the audio tier proved the same way (FR-R246-4), re-resolve if the track set grows (FR-R246-5), **on-device on the reported title before `✓ Built`** (FR-R246-6) — the check R241 left open. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R246-remembered-track-reaches-next-episode.md) |
| R247 | ✓ Built | **Every language in the library gets its name, not its code** — spec'd and **built 2026-09-16** (client-only; `seams/LanguageIdentity.kt` is the one canonicaliser + name table, 12 new tests green, not dev-reviewed, not device-tested; every production DB language code now has a name and endonym, subtitle rows draw a captions glyph, `Dubbed` and R241's memory use canonical identity). Origin: the sweep (F7 + the `Dubbed` item in F14). The picker showed **`EL`**, **`HBS-SRP`**, **`HBS-HRV`** (Greek flag beside the first, a *microphone* beside the other two) and badged an English audio track on an English show **Dubbed**. All one defect: identity by string equality against tables that don't know the strings the player produces — ExoPlayer-normalised BCP-47 for subtitles (`sr` → `hbs-srp`), Jellyfin's 3-letter `eng` for audio, jellystructure's 2-letter `originalLanguage`. `RAVILO_ENDONYMS` and `LANGUAGE_NAMES` (24 entries each) have no Greek/Serbian/Croatian/Hungarian/Romanian/Slovak; **R241's `sameLanguage()` is built on the same table**, so every gap is also a remembered choice that cannot survive a code change. FRs: one `canonicalLanguage()` (639-1/2B/2T + ExoPlayer's macrolanguage and deprecated forms), region kept separately (FR-R247-1); `sameLanguage` hoisted to the seam and used by the resolver, `audioBadges`, `buildLanguageGroups` and the flag strip (FR-R247-2); one name table covering every code the production DB holds (FR-R247-3); a recognised code never shown as a code (FR-R247-4); a captions glyph, never a mic, on a subtitle row (FR-R247-5); `Dubbed` via `sameLanguage` (FR-R247-6). No new flag assets — R239's closed decisions stay closed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R247-every-language-gets-its-name.md) |

| R281 | ✓ Built | **The on-screen keyboard needs a real input to attach to** — reported and built 2026-09-20, not dev-reviewed, **not verified on a real device**. `ravilo-web`'s `CanvasBasedWindow` (kept over the newer `ComposeViewport` deliberately — migrating would disrupt the hand-authored canvas layering, `<video>` backdrop and manual key-dispatch this app already has) routes all text entry through Compose's own focus system on a single canvas, with no DOM `<input>` of its own — so `document.activeElement` was always `#ComposeTarget`, before a tap, after a tap, and after a focus hand-off between two different fields, confirmed empirically under Playwright + Chromium's Pixel 5 emulation against the real production wasmJs build. A mobile browser raises its software keyboard for exactly one thing (a real, focusable, editable DOM element gaining focus), which never existed anywhere on this page; desktop typing was unaffected the whole time, since physical key events land on the canvas regardless. Fix: a hidden, invisible, `pointer-events:none` native `<input id="ravilo-kb-bridge">`, focused/blurred by `boot.js` on coarse-pointer devices only, driven from the exact edge `TextFieldFocusBridge.kt`'s wasmJs actual already reported focus/blur to for an unrelated fullscreen-hotkey guard — one more JS hook call on an edge that already ran on every Compose text-field focus change, no new Kotlin listener. Its `beforeinput`/`keydown` handlers translate what the on-screen keyboard sends into the same synthetic `KeyboardEvent`s the existing gamepad-to-D-pad bridge already fires at the canvas (now hoisted into one shared `raviloFireCanvasKey`), proven (via a bare `new KeyboardEvent` with no Playwright keyboard API involved) to carry arbitrary Unicode text, not just arrow keys; every branch calls `preventDefault()` so the hidden input never becomes a second, drifting copy of the field's text. Composition input (CJK IME) isn't bridged — out of scope for this household's en/da/fo. **Verified**: reverted to pre-fix source, rebuilt the actual production bundle, confirmed the new Playwright test (`tests/e2e/ravilo-web.spec.ts`) fails (`Expected: "INPUT", Received: "CANVAS"`); re-applied the fix, rebuilt again, confirmed it passes, and confirmed by hand (screenshotted) that typing — including a Danish/Faroese-alphabet character and a backspace — reaches the field correctly through the bridge with the hidden input's own value staying empty throughout. **Not verified**: no automated framework (this project's or any other) can observe an OS software keyboard actually rendering; a real-device pass is still what would close this out completely. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R281-the-on-screen-keyboard-needs-a-real-input.md) |

Coil loads images through Ktor's Android engine (`HttpURLConnection`); a recycled grid cell cancels its in-flight download on the main thread, the engine's close drains the body while the IO thread reads it, the platform okio throws inside a cancellation handler, and the process dies — REST calls through R305's `DetachedCalls` hit the same. | R294 | ✓ Built | **An MKV whose track list is at the end plays at once** — written and **built 2026-09-24** (`58a0f552`), not dev-reviewed. **Verified on the stue TV, release build:** the broken episode starts from 0:00 with the decoder up in under 1 s (was: a 33 s full-file read, then a stall loop), one `PlaybackInfo`, no stall, seeking works, a clean episode unchanged; 8/8 Robolectric tests (stock Media3 keeps 0 frames on both broken fixtures, the patched copy keeps all). **Closed 2026-09-24:** acceptance 2 and 4 run on the Pixel 9 (debug build) — the broken episode's decoder up in 0.84–0.98 s vs 0.63 s for a clean one, no stall line, one `PlaybackInfo`, 41 MiB in the first ten seconds (the ordinary buffer fill; a clean episode reads 39), phase 185's samples 1–2 s; FR-R294-6 re-aimed at upstream's own fix (`d386bbf954`, `eb2965ce41`, verified), named in the file header; the factory now forwards `setMatroskaExtractorFlags`. Written Media3's `MatroskaExtractor` follows `SeekHead` only to Cues and discards the `Tracks` entry, so a file with `Tracks` at EOF is read linearly to the end, every frame dropped, then "plays" nothing at 0:00 while R220 re-prepares and re-reads the whole file (~38 s laps on the stue TV). | R288 | ✓ Built | **Faroese says what it means** — design-authored and built 2026-09-21 with the owner answering every terminology question; not dev-reviewed, **not yet on a device**. R287 fixed the spellings the file could *prove* and its STATUS row named what it could not: `Mal`, `Spaling`, `sjalvvirkandi`, `Latid` are stripped **everywhere**, so they are self-consistent and the checker sees nothing, and a stem heuristic was tried and rejected for flagging `Heim` and `samband`, which are correctly unaccented. R288 is the half R287 declared out of scope: **translation quality**. **168 fo strings + 7 da; no key added, removed or reordered.** ⚠ The worst string in the file was not a misspelling at all: `cast.stop` read **`Stovna casting`** — *stovna* is *found/establish*, so **the Stop casting button said start**, in valid JSON with every test green. Also `Sig live` (*say*), `Bryt profil` (*break*), `Fjern` (Danish), `bíða um` (*wait for*) where the Request tab means *biðja um*, and two strings that are not words: **`bogvahandan tínar`** where *your library* belongs (the file says `savnið` correctly four other times) and **`Ofrlinu`**. **Nine terms were drawn 2–5 ways and the owner picked one each** — episode `partur` (was `attrid`·`evni`·`partar`·`rað`·`táttur`), season **`Sesong`** (was `Leikrit`, *a stage play*), collections `Samlingar` (`Savn` meant Library *and* Collections), server `ambætari`, profile `vangi` (8 strings said `vangamynd`, *the photo*, so "this photo cannot see this"), screen `skermur`, device `eind` (was also `telda`, *a computer*), `Sí`, title `heiti`. **The checker had a hole of its own**: `fold()` lowercased *after* `ø`→`o`, so an uppercase `Ø` never folded — which is how `screens.all` sat on `Öll tíni sjónvørp`, a letter Faroese does not have, one line from two keys spelling it `Øll`, through the very check meant to catch that collision and through R287, which fixed `öllum`→`øllum` in the same pass. **`i18n/lexicon/<code>.txt` is the ground truth the rejected heuristic lacked** — every word form reviewed once, so `Mal` fails against `mál` while `Heim` passes; the `ae`/`oe`/`aa` digraph rule rides along (owner: *"never terms like `Naest`, use real letters like `Næst`"*). Six regressions tested, incl. the one R287 could not see — both occurrences of `mál` stripped, file self-consistent and wrong → `"mal" — should be mál`. Found on the way: Faroese `mín` collides with `min`, the **minutes abbreviation**; both correct, `UNIT_WORDS` exempt, named like R287's `A–Á`. English gets no lexicon (`aeon`, `oestrogen`; no accented orthography to protect). **A TV is a `sjónvarp`** (owner: *`TV` only where small space forces an abbreviation*) — 19 of 21 strings already said so; `livetv.guide` `TV-skrá`→`Sjónvarpsskrá` (a 24sp heading and an 18sp panel, and sibling `open_guide` already fits the long form at 16sp), and at the one genuinely narrow spot — the 11sp letter-spaced kicker — the owner **dropped the noun rather than abbreviate it** (`Beinleiðis`). `livetv.live_badge` `LIVE`→`BEINLEIÐIS`. Faroese now has **no bare `TV`**, and `DISCOURAGED` — now carrying a **second owner rule, `telefonurin`** (owner: *the `urin` part on `telefon` is always incorrect*; it is feminine — telefonin/telefonina/telefonini, and the file had it 2-vs-4 with every string reading fine alone) — keeps it that way: tiny, per-language, exception-by-key, empty allow-list, Danish untouched (`dette tv` is ordinary Danish). **Eleven rounds of questions, every candidate found mechanically** — seams worked and exhausted in order: one concept two ways · words appearing once · strings identical to English · long sentences · grammatical person · tense vs the screen's purpose · gender agreement · strings this phase itself authored · fo-vs-da length ratio (**six hits, all benign** — Faroese is just more compound-heavy; recorded as unproductive). ⚠ **One latent defect fixed by luck, not method**: the owner's AirPlay rewrite removed the only two masculine uses of `telefon`, so it is now consistently feminine like the other four — the same defect as `app`, which nobody had raised. ⚠ **An automated agreement scan was written and is NOT kept** — noun genders taken from the file's own unambiguous uses, matched against every preceding determiner. Across 421 strings it produced **exactly one hit, and the hit was wrong**: `allar vangar` is correct because the noun is accusative, where masculine and feminine share the form, and the scan had no notion of case (owner: *`allar` kann vera bæði kallkyn og kvennkyn tá tað er hvønnfall fleirtal*). R287's rejected stem heuristic exactly — a rule with no grasp of the thing it judges produces confident noise. `Nýggjar filmar` was caught by a person reading a row title. **`app` is feminine** — the file had `appinum`/`appini`/`appina`, three forms and two genders, each fine in isolation. **Agreement, the class no rule reaches** — every word spelled correctly, the wrong form chosen, all found by putting candidates to the owner: `row.new_movies` `Nýggjar filmar` (feminine adjective on the masculine `filmur`, while its sibling `Nýggjar seriur` is right because `seria` IS feminine) → `Nýggjir filmar`; `detail.episodes_watched` `pørtum sædd`→`sæddir`, wrong from the moment `partur` was chosen **by this phase**; `browse.maturity.any` `Øll`→`Einki mark` (English is *Any*, not *All* — and the owner corrected the gender of my own suggestion, `Eingin`→`Einki`); `cast.lost_sub`'s feminine `Hon` for a TV → `Sjónvarpið`, and `srv.busy_sub` lost its pronoun entirely. **Cast stays English by owner decision** — `Cast til {device}`, beside `Steðga casting`. Kept: `Tilpassa`/`Fyll`, `Strika filtur`, `screens.code_hint`, `settings.unpair_desc`. **Last sweep, by three new seams** — words appearing exactly **once** (nothing corroborates them), strings still **identical to English**, and **long sentences**: `Setan` (a session noun used nowhere else) rewritten away entirely → `Henda eindin er ikki innritað longur.`; the English `feeds` inside a Faroese sentence → `keldur`; `Onga lýsing enn` → `Eingin lýsing enn` (accusative where its own siblings use the nominative); `Skift fullan skerm` → `Slá fullan skerm til/frá` (*Toggle* had lost its on/off). Kept: `Admin`, `Trailer`, `Sendidagur`, and `Fekk ikki samband` — one English sentence rendered three ways **on purpose**, since R237's player errors avoid naming a component the viewer cannot see. **The Upcoming rail could not tell the future from the past** — six labels for things that have NOT happened used the past participle (*Airs today* = `Sent í dag`, on the same rail as `Sent í gjár` for *Aired yesterday*: identical construction, distinguished only by the day word). The file already knew the future form (`sonarr.airs` = `verður sendur`), so it was a slip, not a gap. Owner split it by kind — an episode is broadcast, a film comes out: airs `Kemur út`/`Kom út`, releases `Verður útgivið`/`Varð útgivið`; three more strings carried the retired verbs and followed. **`player.badge_signs_only` was `Bara skilti`** — *skilti* is signage, not subtitles-for-signs, and `bara` was the file's only one where every other string says `bert` — now `Bert fremmant mál`. Kept: `Reinsa`, `Angra`. **A second owner pass, by question rather than read-through** (owner: *"I don't want to read through it. Keep asking me questions"*) — candidates found mechanically as *one English concept rendered two ways*: `Leikfólk`/`Luttakarar`→`Leikarar og framleiðsla`/`Leikarar`; `Varðgeym`/`Vista`/`goymt`→**`Goym`**; `Sleppa intro`→**`Skip intro`** (owner: *not properly translatable, and `Skip intro` is valid Faroese*) with `Skip eftirtekstirnar` to match; `Bið`→**`Umbøn`** (a bare imperative among four nouns). Kept after review and recorded so they are not re-raised: `Góðska`, `Uppdaga`, `Studio`, `Steðga casting`, and the `Ljóðlýsing`/`Sjónlýsing` pair. **Renaming *Networks* pulled a thread**: a broadcaster was `Støðir` ×3 but `Rás` in `up.network`, a live-TV channel was `rás` ×2 / `sjónvarpsstøð` ×2 / `støð` ×1, and a Ravilo collection was also `Rás` — so `rás` meant three things and `støð` two. Now broadcaster=`sjónvarpsrás`, live channel=`rás`, studio=`Studio`, collection=`Samling`/`Savn`; `støð` is gone and `seg.studios`' note (which still said *"Støðir for networks"*) now names all four and says not to merge them. ⚠ `browse.facet.channel` is `Savn`, knowingly re-overlapping `nav.library`. ⚠ `action.save` is drawn by **no client** — a third word for *save* no viewer could see. **`player.next`/`pl.next`/`player.up_next` `Næsta`→`Næsti`** — the next-episode button carried the feminine/oblique form of *næstur* while `row.next_up` and `sonarr.next_ep` already said `Næsti partur`, so three strings disagreed with the two others naming the same thing (episode = `partur`, masculine). The file's three other *næstur* forms are each correct and stay: `næsta part` (acc masc), `næstu ferð` (fem), `Næst` (adverbial, live Now/Next). **`seriar`→`seriur`** (4 strings) — and the file had already contradicted itself: `serium` (dat pl) and `seriuna` (def acc sg) are only correct on a `seriu-` stem, so one paradigm was inflecting two ways with every word spelled correctly. **`lib.type.all` `Øll`→`Alt`** — the media-type pill's *All*, whose two sibling chips (`browse.all`, `up.all`) already said `Alt`; `Øll` now survives only in `screens.all` (`Øll tíni sjónvørp`), where it agrees with the noun. ⚠ `browse.maturity.any` is also a standalone `Øll` but its English is **Any**, in the maturity range rows — raised, not guessed. **`loading` `Løðir…`→`Heintar…`** (owner; `Løðir` came from R264's Tizen table and now appears nowhere) — owner's caveat recorded, not resolved: *loading may want different words in different contexts*, but **one key serves eight render sites**, so that is a key split argued in English first, not a translation. **One grammatical person for a progressive**: Faroese inflects for person where English and Danish do not, and **15 of 17** progressive strings are 3rd person — so `loading.still_trying` `Royni`→`Roynir` (it stacks **10dp under `loading`** in PlayerScreen's cold start, so Faroese read *Heintar… / Royni framvegis…*, 3rd then 1st, in one glance; `receiver.setup_trying` already had `Roynir at sambinda…`) and `home.showing_saved`, which contradicted *itself* (`Vísir` … `royni`). ⚠ R288's own first pass moved `home.showing_saved` from `royna` to `royni` **to match the outlier** — found only because the owner asked what `loading` says. **The lexicon cannot see this class**: every word is spelled correctly; agreement is not spelling and nothing checks it. ⚠ **The last four findings share a shape** — `Øll`, `Royni`, `seriar`, `Næsta` — *every word spelled correctly, the wrong word or wrong form chosen*. Noir drops the tint on both L and J per FR-R240-12, matching R221/R222's precedent. Four new strings (`fd.audio`/`fd.subs`/`fd.nodesc`/`fd.min_left`) × en/da/fo (FR-R240-11) — the season/episode count phrase ("3 Seasons · 24 episodes") is left as hardcoded English on purpose, matching the *design mockup's own* unlocalized `ravilo-app.js` count string this phase mirrors, not a new gap. 8 new `FocusDetailControllerTest` cases (real-clock dwell timing, wide margins against scheduler jitter — this module has no `kotlinx-coroutines-test` dependency); `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest`/`allTests` all green. **FR-R240-9 built 2026-09-12 as its own mechanism** (was previously simplified onto R108/R140's `BringIntoViewRequester`): `focusDetailRowOpenScrollDelta()` (new, pure — `FocusDetailScroll.kt`) implements the spec's literal formula `max(rowTop−150, rowFoot+40−screenHeight)`, wired into `ContentRowItem` via `Modifier.onGloballyPositioned` capturing the row's own top/foot in window space, read once — deliberately delayed by `ROW_OPEN_TWEEN_MS` so the read lands after the row's held-height ratchet (FR-R240-8) has actually settled, never mid-tween (R232's hazard) — then `LazyListState.animateScrollBy(delta)` only when `delta > 0f` (a row that already fits is never pulled, satisfying "never scrolls back up"). 5 new `FocusDetailScrollTest` cases on the pure formula (top-constraint-wins / foot-constraint-wins / already-fits / zero-viewport). **FR-R240-10 built 2026-09-12: `StaticContentRow` now holds two simultaneous panel slots.** A new `closingAfterKey`/`closingPanel` pair sits beside the existing `openAfterKey`/`openPanel` (`ContentRow.kt`); a lateral hop from one open tile straight to another in the SAME row moves the previously-open tile's panel into the closing slot (rendered permanently `visible = false`, so its own `shrinkHorizontally` exit tween plays out undisturbed) while the newly-focused tile's panel expands in the open slot — two `FocusDetailPanel` instances genuinely mid-tween at once instead of one shared node being silently re-anchored. `ContentRowItem`'s state machine (`HomeScreen.kt`) splits into two effects: the outer one (keyed on `rowHasOpen`/`fd?.itemKey`) detects a hop and shuffles `panelKey`/`panelUi` into `closingKey`/`closingUi`, and an inner one scoped to `closingKey` itself schedules that slot's own delayed clear — decoupled from the outer effect's own restarts so a second hop mid-tween cleanly replaces the closing slot rather than leaving an orphaned cleanup coroutine. `panelUi` (the open slot's snapshot) is now updated only inside the effect, never unconditionally from `fd`, specifically so the previous tile's facts survive long enough to be handed off to the closing slot. Compose's own `LazyRow` per-item-key anchor tracking reflows both tiles' width changes without any manual scroll-delta subtraction — the `FD.hShift` catch-up math the original HTML mockup needed doesn't have an equivalent gap here, so nothing further to build on that front. **Known simplification, documented rather than silently dropped:** a THIRD hop landing before the second hop's own exit tween finishes overwrites the closing slot outright (no third slot) — a rapid-fire edge case beyond what the two-slot spec asks for. `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest`/`allTests` and root `:compileKotlinWasmJs` all clean; no new pure logic to unit-test (the mechanism is Compose state/effect wiring, not a pure function like FR-R240-9's), so verification here is compile+existing-suite only — **not device-tested**, per standing no-TV-testing-without-asking agreement. Acceptance tests 3 and 6 from the earlier note remain UNVERIFIED on a real device. Open question 1 (reflow cost on the stue BRAVIA) remains open; J still ships off by Phase 202's own default. **Open question 3 (reduced motion) resolved and built 2026-09-12**: `effectiveFocusDetailMode()` (new, pure, `FocusDetailReducedMotion.kt`) downgrades a resolved `"rowOpen"` to `"line"` whenever `systemPrefersReducedMotion()` (new seam — Android: `ValueAnimator.areAnimatorsEnabled()`/API 26+ falling back to reading `Settings.Global.ANIMATOR_DURATION_SCALE` below that; wasmJs: `prefers-reduced-motion` media query) reports it, applied at both call sites into `FocusDetailController` (the household-level reveal/reserved-band effect in `HomeLoaded` and the per-tile `onFocus` in `ContentRowItem`) so the two can't disagree about which mode is really active. 4 new `FocusDetailReducedMotionTest` cases; `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`compileKotlinWasmJs`/`testDebugUnitTest`/`allTests` all green. **Device-tested 2026-09-12 on the stue TV (BRAVIA 4K VH21)**, release build, AOT-compiled, against the real household config (`focusDetailRowOpen` flipped on by the app owner for this pass, reverted after): J's settle/open, the FR-R240-10 lateral-hop crossfade, and the reduced-motion downgrade (confirmed live via `animator_duration_scale=0` — a fresh launch rendered L instead of J for the identical config) all behave as designed. `dumpsys gfxinfo` on a single settled row-open: 19 frames / 1 janky (5.26%) / 1 deadline miss, GPU flat at 4ms; a 5-press rapid sweep produced only 53 frames / 1 janky (1.89%) with the dwell correctly suppressing every intermediate open — one real, encouraging data point for open question 1, not the systematic sweep-and-trace invariant 11 still asks for, so J still ships off by default. Acceptance tests 3, 4 and 6 confirmed; 5, 7, 9, 10 not tested this pass. **Three live-reported J bugs, all root-caused to ONE mistake and fixed 2026-09-13 — measuring the panel to decide where to scroll is circular.** (1) A panel opened near a row's right edge rendered entirely off-screen; (2) the first fix for it clipped the opening tile's own poster off the LEFT edge, on some rows but not others; (3) after a fast repeated-press sweep, no panel appeared at all until any later keypress revealed it. Root cause: a `LazyRow` never *places* an item outside its viewport and `onGloballyPositioned` only fires for placed nodes — J's panel sits to the RIGHT of a tile that is frequently already at the right edge, so exactly when it most needs scrolling in, it is unplaced and unmeasurable. Device logging proved every other layer was correct (dwell fires, state machine transitions, the panel node genuinely composes) and disproved three successive theories on-device (dwell-cancellation race, recomposition storm starving `AnimatedVisibility`, a swallowed `CancellationException`). Fix: `focusDetailRowOpenTargetScrollDelta()` (new, pure) computes the target from KNOWN geometry only — the focused tile's own `layoutInfo` offset/width, FR-R240-7's `ROW_OPEN_WIDTH_SCALE` for its grown size, and the newly-exported `FOCUS_DETAIL_PANEL_WIDTH` constant — so nothing needs laying out first; it animates on the SAME `tween(ROW_OPEN_TWEEN_MS)` the tile and panel use (one motion, not grow-pause-slide); and the result is **clamped at the tile's own offset so the opening tile can never be scrolled past the row's content start**, which is bug (2)'s fix (the per-row variation was `TileVariant.LANDSCAPE` in Continue Watching vs `POSTER` elsewhere — different widths, different overscroll). The measuring `Box`, `onGloballyPositioned` and `snapshotFlow` correction loop are all deleted rather than worked around. 6 new `FocusDetailRowOpenTargetScrollTest` cases (one asserts the unclamped arithmetic *would* have clipped, so the clamp can't silently regress); 27 focus-detail tests green. **A fourth bug, reported in the same pass and fixed with it: the panel's own width was a hardcoded 620dp that doesn't fit.** On this house's 960dp-wide TV a grown LANDSCAPE tile (256→366dp) leaves only 482dp, so the synopsis was cut off mid-word on the right — while a grown POSTER tile (155→221dp) leaves 627dp and cleared 620dp by 7dp, which is why it clipped in Continue Watching and nowhere else and read as intermittent. `focusDetailPanelWidthFor(variant)` now derives it as `screen − gutters − grownTile − spacing` (482/627/591dp for LANDSCAPE/POSTER/SQUARE, each summing to exactly 960dp), passed to `StaticContentRow` as `openPanelWidth` so the scroll maths and the panel it scrolls share one number; the panel's two horizontal lines (badges, flags+resume) became `FlowRow`s so they can't overflow the narrower panel either. **Device-verified on the stue TV** across every previously-failing shape: fast sweep, LANDSCAPE and POSTER rows, the FR-R240-10 lateral hop, and full untruncated synopsis text. **Open question 1 (reflow-cost invariant 11) closed 2026-09-13**: a systematic sweep-and-trace pass on the stue TV (still AOT-compiled, `[status=speed]` reconfirmed) measured 46 real settled row-opens across both tile shapes plus a zero-frame idle baseline and a 25-press rapid sweep — 2.8-3.2% janky frames per settled open, 0 missed vsync ever, and the FR-R240-6 dwell fully absorbing rapid-navigation cost (0.35% jank). J's default flips from off to on (Phase 202's `focusDetailRowOpen` default `false` → `true`); the household's live config already had it on independently. Not deployed to prod as part of this pass (a default-value change needs a backend rebuild + restart). Not dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R240-focus-detail-on-home-rows.md) |

The correct rule was already written in the comment three lines above (*"whichever one is CURRENTLY playing"*) and never expressed in code; `selectedFlat` was already computed at the call site (`:2335`) and simply not passed down. Same shape as R202 and R231 — a comment stating an invariant the code doesn't implement. Fixed by extracting a pure `languageRowBadges(group, selectedFlat)` (testable, and keeps the composable from growing — see R237's `VerifyError`): selected version's badges → else the only version's → else none (FR-R238-2: nothing beats wrong). Applies to the audio tab too (FR-R238-3, shared row). Acceptance #2 (switching to Signs only updates the row) is unit-tested only — deliberately not exercised live, since an explicit pick is remembered per-series by R181/R195 and a mis-step would leave the household with signs-only subtitles remembered. | R237 | ✓ Built | **The player spends 15 seconds retrying a failure that cannot succeed** — spec'd 2026-09-06 from the same live report as Phase 194 (*"it didn't start fast enough, so I stopped"*). The viewer saw a spinner and nothing else. Server-side each of the three Play presses was an immediate `409` — *"This device's Jellyfin sign-in has expired"* — a deterministic answer that would not change for ten minutes. `PlayerStore.startSession` retries `repeat(5)` with 1/2/4/8 s backoff — **~15 s of unbroken spinner** — and never looks at *why* it failed: `result.exceptionOrNull()?.message` discards a status code the client already holds, since `assertSuccess` throws `TvApiError.Http(status, body)` and `TvApiError.Http` exposes `val status: Int`. So all five attempts were guaranteed to fail identically before the first was sent. The viewer backed out after 4 s and 6 s; **the re-pair message was rendered zero times** — a precise diagnosis reached the client and was shown to nobody. The comment's stated intent (survive an auto-advance blip quietly) is right for a transport error and wrong for every 4xx. Fixes: classify before retrying — 409/403/404 terminal, transport/408/429/5xx retryable, honouring `Retry-After` (FR-R237-1); replace *"Something went wrong"* over a raw `HTTP 409: {json}` with per-cause copy naming no product or protocol, en/da/fo (FR-R237-2); Retry only where retrying can work (FR-R237-3); auto-advance stops on a terminal error instead of walking the season one 409 at a time (FR-R237-4); one additive *"Still trying…"* line on R218's existing cold-start treatment at ~5 s, since its 60 s deepen is far past the measured 4 s give-up point (FR-R237-5); and a `startFailureStatus` on the QoE report so the next incident is readable from the database alone (FR-R237-6). Worth building even after 194 lands: 194 removes this cause, R237 removes the class of symptom. **Built 2026-09-06 alongside 194, all six FRs.** `classifyStartFailure` splits terminal from retryable; `TvApiError.Http` gained `retryAfterSeconds` (read in `assertSuccess`, honoured in place of the computed backoff, ignored outside 0–60 s). `PlayerSessionState.Error` carries a `PlayerErrorKind`, and `PlayerScreen` renders per-cause copy — nine strings × en/da/fo, none naming a product, protocol or status code. Retry appears only where retrying can work; `REAUTH` gets a **Sign in** action wired to the same exit R234's forced sign-out uses, which answers the spec's open question 2 (leave the player entirely rather than layer R175's login takeover over a dead one). **FR-R237-4 needed no code** — auto-advance is already latched to one attempt per episode and a screen whose session errored never reaches `player.isEnded`, so the season cannot be walked one 409 at a time. **Open question 1 answered: `ravilo-tizen` does not share this loop** (single attempt, `finish()` on failure — no retry, but also no message; a separate smaller gap, left alone per R190's per-target precedent). FR-R237-6 adds `startFailureStatus` to the QoE report and `playback_qoe` (migration `42.sqm`), so the next incident is readable from the DB alone. Incidental fix: the loading overlay's children were direct children of a centre-aligned `Box`, so its 48 dp spacer did nothing and *"Loading…"* drew on top of the spinner — a `Column` now. Six new `PlayerStartFailureClassificationTest` cases; all six compile targets + `testDebugUnitTest` green. **⚠ The first build of this phase crashed the player outright and was caught on the stue TV the same day**: `java.lang.VerifyError: Verifier rejected class …PlayerScreen… register v2 has type Precise Reference: java.lang.String but expected Integer` — ART refused to load the class, so opening any title killed the app before a single playback request reached the server. Release-build only (R8-minified); every compile target and unit test passed. The obvious suspect — FR-R237-3's new `onReauthRequired` parameter making `PlayerScreen` a 16-argument composable — was **not** the cause: moving it to a `LocalReauthRequired` CompositionLocal just moved the verifier failure to a different register. The real cause is `PlayerScreen`'s size (~3,500 lines); inlining the new error card pushed its Compose-generated method past what the verifier accepts. Proved by reverting only this phase's client files, which restored playback. Fixed by extracting `PlayerSessionErrorOverlay` into its own composable (the CompositionLocal is kept — it stops the argument list growing again). **Re-verified on stue TV**: player opens, picture renders, crash buffer empty. Standing constraint recorded in the spec — new player chrome goes in its own `@Composable`, never inlined into `PlayerScreen`. Not dev-reviewed. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R237-dont-retry-a-failure-that-cannot-succeed.md) |
| R236 | ✓ Built | **Down from the Home hero can stop working entirely** — spec'd 2026-09-06 from a live report on stue TV (*"sometimes I can't get below the carousel, even though I can see everything below it… I'm not sure if this only happens when doing something else first, like opening a movie and go back"*). The reporter's own hedge is the clue. The hero consumes Down and hands focus to `firstRowFR`, which is attached to exactly one thing: **tile index 0 of the first row** — a `LazyRow` item inside a `LazyColumn`. A lazy list disposes items outside its composed window, and `requestFocus()` on an unattached requester throws; `requestFocusRetrying` swallows it, retries 30 frames and gives up, so the key press does nothing and so does every one after it. `dpadFocusable`'s own doc already forbids this (*"never one-per-item across a lazy list"*) and `HomeScreen.kt:187-189` claims compliance — the requester is single, but it is not always-composed. Two ways the target disappears: **R139's Back-return restore scrolls the row to the tile you opened**, disposing index 0 (exactly the reporter's *"open a movie and go back"*), and plain Right-arrow navigation does the same; plus a narrower window where `fr ?: if (i == 0) firstRowFR` leaves the bridge with no target at all during a restore. R200's retry can't help — the target isn't late, it's absent. Fix: the bridge targets the **row container** carrying `focusGroup()`, letting `StaticContentRow`'s existing `Modifier.focusRestorer()` compose-and-scroll its remembered child — the one thing a per-item requester provably cannot do (FR-R236-1); Down from the hero falls through to native focus search rather than consuming the key on failure (FR-R236-2); same for the AppBar bridge (FR-R236-3); plus a guard for the class, which has now produced R200, R201, R223, R232 and the 2026-09-05 Settings fix (FR-R236-5). Built same day: `StaticContentRow` gained a `rowFocusRequester` param wired to the `LazyRow` itself via `focusRequester()+focusRestorer()` (no separate `focusGroup()` needed — `focusRestorer()` is self-sufficient per the actual Compose UI 1.9.4 sources); every per-item `fr ?: if (i==0) firstRowFR` conditional in `HomeScreen.kt` is gone. A new `requestFocusRetryingOrMoveNative` falls through to native `FocusManager.moveFocus` when every retry fails, since `dpadFocusable`'s `onDown` always consumes the key once supplied. The same per-item pattern was found (not fixed) in `ChannelScreen.kt`/`SeededBrowseScreen.kt`/`BrowseScreen.kt` — flagged as a follow-up, not folded in. `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`testDebugUnitTest` green. Not reproduced on-device (no-TV-without-asking); two deterministic repro steps given. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R236-home-hero-down-strands-focus.md) |
| R235 | ✓ Built | **Never auto-select a signs-only subtitle track when a real one exists** — spec'd 2026-09-06 from a live report on stue TV (*"there is no subtitles, but the picker says danish is selected; I have to go in and pick the other danish one"*). **Root cause confirmed against the exact reported file.** `Kulsort.S07E03` carries two Danish subtitle tracks: `Dansk` (**forced = true, default = true**) and `Dansk (CC)` (plain). Danish audio + a Danish *forced* track means an almost-empty screen by design — and every branch of `resolveTrackChoice` lands on it: a remembered language whose variant signature doesn't match this file falls back to `native.firstOrNull()` (stream order → the forced one), no memory at all falls to `firstOrNull { it.isDefault }` (the file says the forced one is default), and the final fallback `firstOrNull { it.forced }` prefers forced outright. Nothing in the ladder knows that a forced track is not a substitute for a full one. **Not one bad file: 247 of 3 476 subtitled units (7.1 %) of the live library** have a language whose first-or-default subtitle track is forced while a non-forced sibling exists — *Kulsort*, *Efterforskningen*, Marvel and Netflix releases alike. R181 resolved by language, R195 refined to language+variant but only on an exact match; neither has a concept of a track being insufficient on its own. Fix: within a language prefer *exact remembered variant → first non-forced → anything* (FR-R235-1); the source's `default` flag can't override that (FR-R235-2); an explicit pick of Signs only is still honoured and still remembered forever (FR-R235-3); the same PLAIN-preferred rule for audio, where commentary/audio-description are the equivalent (FR-R235-4); and `SDH_RE` learns `cc` / `closed caption`, which the reported file's own title uses (FR-R235-5). Client-only — the scanned track metadata was correct all along. Built same day, all six FRs, six new `PlayerScreenTrackResolutionTest` cases incl. the exact Kulsort shape; `:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`/`testDebugUnitTest` green. Not device-tested. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R235-never-auto-select-a-signs-only-subtitle.md) |

One crop rule everywhere, circles except the profile picker's rounded-square tile (FR-R234-9), and en/da/fo at ship (FR-R234-10). **Both provisional controls resolved 2026-09-05 by 187's probe and an owner decision:** preset colours are **dropped** (the deterministic initials gradient stands; FR-R234-3 was already written to survive their removal, and the `photo_presets` string comes out), and FR-R234-7 is **unblocked** — a password change does not invalidate tokens, so it shows *"Password changed."*, returns to Settings, signs nobody out and says nothing about other devices. | R223 | ✓ Built | **Detail screen: invisible season-picker focus, and rapid Up stranding the scroll below the hero** — design-authored 2026-09-02, built 2026-09-03, not dev-reviewed, not re-verified live against the built APK. **Deployed to both TVs same session** (BRAVIA 4K VH21 / stue TV + BRAVIA 4K VH2), installed + AOT-compiled, neither launched. From a live UX pass on stue TV, triggered by two standing reports: "trouble switching between seasons" (Blå Blink) and the top-of-page scroll not working after navigating around. Both reproduced live with screenshots. **Bug 1**: `SeasonPicker.kt`'s focus border is gated `focused && !isSelected` — the instant a pill becomes selected (i.e. right after the viewer picks it) its only strong focus indicator vanishes, leaving just a hard-to-read scale/glow (confirmed via pixel diff); a same-hue border can't fix it either, since `focusRing` sits deliberately close to `accent` in every skin. Fix: border driven by `focused` alone, colored `onAccent` when selected (reusing the pill's own existing selected-text contrast pattern) vs. `focusRing` when not. **Bug 2**: two independent "go up" mechanisms coexist on both detail screens — `upToHero` (season picker / lone-season episode rail) explicitly scrolls the list to item 0 and focuses Play; three other paths (genre row, synopsis, and the Play-row's own Up handler) send focus straight to the nav bar with **no scroll at all**, assuming an earlier scroll already landed. A fast burst of Up presses (live-reproduced: 15 rapid presses from the page bottom) can outrun the async `scrollToItem(0)`, landing focus on the nav bar — an overlay outside the `LazyColumn` that nothing can "bring into view" — while the list is stuck mid-scroll, hiding the title and top of the backdrop indefinitely; only Down-from-navbar (a non-obvious recovery) fixes it. Fix: route all three bare `navBarFR.requestFocus()` call sites on **both** `SeriesDetailScreen.kt` and `MovieDetailScreen.kt` (identical structure, movies have no `upToHero` at all) through a shared `goToNavBar()` helper that always re-issues `scrollToItem(0)`, so the last Up event in any burst — however fast — converges on the correct end state regardless of timing. Compiles clean across all 5 Ravilo targets incl. `:ravilo-android:assembleRelease`. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R223-detail-focus-scroll-bugs.md) |

| R220 | ✓ Built | **✓ Built 2026-08-31 (FR-R220-1/2/3/6) + 2026-09-02 (FR-R220-4/5) — not dev-reviewed, not reproduced on-device, not deployed anywhere (no device access either session; live TVs are now off-limits entirely per standing instruction).** Compiles clean across every affected target (`:ravilo-ui:compileDebugKotlinAndroid`, `:ravilo-ui:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-web:compileKotlinWasmJs`, `:ravilo-android:compileDebugKotlin`, `:ravilo-phone:compileDebugKotlin`); `:ravilo-ui:testDebugUnitTest` passes (pre-existing tests only, none written for this phase). Built the full ladder ahead of FR-R220-1's on-device confirmation rather than narrowed to a confirmed-necessary subset. New `RaviloPlayerAndroid.renderedVideoFrameCount()` reads `exo.videoDecoderCounters.renderedOutputBufferCount`; a 500ms poll loop **kept entirely inside `PlayerVideoSurface`'s Android actual** (not threaded through the common `PlayerScreen` loop as originally sketched — simpler, matches "Android only") declares a stall after ~2s of playing+not-buffering+not-seeking+zero new frames, then runs rungs 1-3 (reattach the captured `SurfaceView` via new `clearVideoSurfaceView()`+`setVideoSurfaceView()` → `seekTo(positionMs)` flush → `key(surfaceGeneration)`-forced `AndroidView` recreation) before rung 4 hands off via a new `onVideoOutputStuck` callback (common `PlayerVideoSurface` signature change, default no-op) that `PlayerScreen` wires to `armSession(currentItemId)` — the same re-arm `onForeground` already uses. New `PlayerQoeSnapshot.videoOutputRecoveries` (additive, defaults 0 elsewhere) counts ladder firings. **FR-R220-4/5 closed 2026-09-02**: a `SurfaceHolder.Callback` registered alongside Media3's own (idempotent redundant re-attach on `surfaceCreated`), an `onWindowVisibilityChanged` override on the `SurfaceView` as a second trigger for the "neither callback fires" TV-standby case, and deterministic `ON_STOP`/`ON_START` detach/re-attach via two new `RaviloPlayer` methods (`detachVideoSurfaceForBackground`/`reattachVideoSurfaceForForeground`, backed by a `currentSurfaceView` ref the actual now tracks itself) wired into `PlayerLifecycleEffect`'s androidMain actual. FR-R220-5: `PlayerVideoSurface` gained `onVideoOutputRecovering: (Boolean) -> Unit`, true for the ladder's duration, folded into `PlayerScreen`'s `rawBufferMoment` ahead of `isSeeking`/`isBuffering` so R218's existing STALL presentation now force-shows for any rung running past the ~400ms debounce. `LiveTvPlayerScreen` shares the same `PlayerVideoSurface` composable so it inherits detection/ladder/prevention for free, but not rung 4 or the forced STALL (no Live TV buffer-moment machinery exists to force). Still no test coverage (same rationale as before — a fake `RaviloPlayer`/`SurfaceView` harness felt like scope creep ahead of real on-device signal). See the spec's own build note and open questions #4/#7 for the full account. **Video output lost on return from background: detect it, recover from it, never sit black** — reported 2026-08-31: pressing Home, or TV standby/resume, then reopening Ravilo — *"the video continues (which is correct). But while audio works perfectly, the video is just black."* The symptom is unusually informative: **audio continuing across the round-trip proves the activity was not recreated** (a recreation rebuilds `ExoPlayer` and audio would restart, not continue), so the same player, the same composition and the **same `SurfaceView` instance** are alive on return — which eliminates most candidates and points at the renderer→surface path. The manifest agrees: `configChanges` (`AndroidManifest.xml:26`) absorbs `screenSize|uiMode|orientation|screenLayout|smallestScreenSize`, so HDMI re-negotiation on resume does not recreate the activity. Findings: (1) **the surface is bound exactly once and never re-bound** — `PlayerVideoSurface.kt` binds inside `AndroidView(factory = …)` with **no `update` block, no key, no re-attach**, and `RaviloPlayerAndroid` exposes only `setVideoSurfaceView` (`:247`) — no `clearVideoSurface`, no detach. The app relies entirely on Media3's internal `SurfaceHolder.Callback`, with **no second line of defence and no way to know it failed**. (2) **Nothing detects "playing but not rendering"** — R218's three moments (`PlayerScreen.kt:876-883`) are `!hasRenderedFirstFrame → COLD`, `isSeeking → SEEK`, `isBuffering → STALL`, and in this failure **all three read healthy** (`STATE_READY`, no seek, and `_hasRenderedFirstFrame` is reset only in `load()` — `RaviloPlayerAndroid.kt:205` — so it stays `true` for the rest of the item once episode 1 has drawn a frame). R218 therefore correctly shows nothing, and the viewer gets a silent black screen with no overlay, no error and no retry. (3) **The lifecycle handler ignores video entirely** — `PlayerLifecycleEffect.kt`'s four events all concern the backend session (R180/R192) or the OS MediaSession (R192); the transition most likely to break video output is the only one with no video handling in it. (4) **TV standby may deliver no lifecycle events at all** (display layer torn down underneath a never-stopped app), so a lifecycle-only fix cannot cover the second reported trigger — which is why the detector is specified in terms of **observed rendering**, not lifecycle transitions. Plan: FR-R220-1 reproduce first and establish which of three cases holds (callbacks fire + no frames / callbacks fire + no re-attach / no callbacks at all) before building rungs we may not need; FR-R220-2 a health signal on the existing 500 ms `POLL_MS` loop — playing **and** position advancing **and** not buffering/seeking **and** no frame rendered for ~2 s, using a real frame *count* (`getVideoDecoderCounters().renderedOutputBufferCount`, verify it updates on the Media3 in use) rather than a boolean, biased conservative since a false positive costs a needless recovery; FR-R220-3 an escalating ladder — re-attach → `seekTo(currentPosition)` flush → recreate the `SurfaceView` → re-prepare at position (what the viewer does by hand today), stopping at the first rung that restores frames; FR-R220-4 deterministic prevention (our own `SurfaceHolder.Callback` re-attaching idempotently, explicit detach on `ON_STOP` / attach on `ON_START`, `onWindowVisibilityChanged` as a fallback trigger); FR-R220-5 invisible under R218's existing ~400 ms debounce, then R218's **STALL** presentation with its already-translated string — no new copy, no new visual language — falling through to the real player-error path if rung 4 fails; FR-R220-6 record which rung fired, so if rung 4 always wins the other three get deleted and if rung 1 always wins the prevention isn't working. Scope is Android only (`ravilo-web` uses a DOM `<video>`, `ravilo-tizen` its own player). Non-goals: changing R218's existing moments, pausing on background instead of continuing, and reverting R173's `SurfaceView` decision. ⚠ Open question flagged for dev review: **Phase 180's session teardown is adjacent, new and in this exact path** — `onBackground` → `stopSession` now genuinely releases an in-flight transcode, and `onForeground`'s `armSession` re-negotiates without re-preparing the player; audio continuing for minutes argues against it being the cause here, but it would be a *different* bug with a different fix and should be confirmed rather than assumed. Design-authored 2026-08-31 from a source read; not yet dev-reviewed and **not yet reproduced on-device**. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R220-video-output-loss-recovery.md) |

Rather than another point patch, built one canonical merged Continue Watching list — membership rules, conflict resolution, cache shape and the touched-window length (corrected from a proposed 30 days to **7 days** mid-Q&A) all settled together before code. **THE RULE, stated prominently in the spec's own top section after being violated once mid-build**: jellystructure merges/sorts/filters/caps — Jellyfin does not; every Jellyfin fetch must be COMPLETE, `Limit` is never a cap (this exact rule was broken 4 separate times across R186/R217/this build, all fixed here). A pre-code review against channel-scoped "See all" (owner insisted on review before "let's build", twice) found a real gap (`continueWatchingAll`/`/tv/continue/all` had no channel param at all) and, while writing that fix into the spec, a **factual error in the reviewer's own first draft** — an invented `"library"` config value that doesn't exist (real value is `"all"`) and a missed inherit-mode-channel third case — corrected before any code was written. Moment A (session negotiation) unchanged; **B (cold start)** gets Direction B "Grounded": black, three-dot brand pulse, title context, indeterminate sweep, the existing `"Loading…"` string; **C (stall)** keeps the frozen frame and raises the transport chrome by itself, a spinner standing in the play button's place — no centre overlay, no text, so the viewer keeps their position; **D (seek)** is lightest: a spinner inside the scrub tile only. **~400ms debounce on all three** (a direct play must never flash), deepens at 60s without changing a word, one already-translated string, no escape hatch (Back is always available, never prompted, ends the session — this is R180's own promise, which R218 only half-delivered until R180 closed the server-side gap the same evening). | R216 | Implemented | **Player must report what it can decode and receive, and record how playback actually went** — client half of **Phase 177**/**178** (see the [investigation](specs/research-reports/stue-tv-4k-playback-stutter-2026-08-28.md)). Three gaps, each the same shape as the "just ask the client" fixes that preceded it (R183's `maxH264*`, Phase 161's `supportsEmbeddedTextSubs`): **(1)** `detectAvcDecoderLimits()` (`androidMain/…/HdrCapabilities.kt:77-93`) already walks `MediaCodecList` and reads `VideoCapabilities` width/height/level but never `getBitrateRange()`, and only for H.264 — stue TV's real **60 Mbps** ceiling sits in an API we already call and is discarded; **(2)** nothing reports the network link, so a 2.4 GHz association at 130 Mbps looks identical to 5 GHz at 585 Mbps; **(3)** ExoPlayer tracks dropped frames and rebuffers natively and Ravilo attaches no `AnalyticsListener`, so every stutter report becomes a forensic reconstruction days later. Plan: FR-R216-1 generalises the decoder probe to HEVC+DV and reports the ceiling **of the decoder that would actually be selected** (never the max across all, or a software fallback masks the hardware limit), `0` when unknown; FR-R216-2 adds `detectLinkState()` (Android `ConnectivityManager` + `WifiInfo.frequency`, the signal that would have caught `f=2462`), `unknown` elsewhere, **no new runtime permission**; FR-R216-3 supplies an explicit `DefaultLoadControl` — raising `bufferForPlaybackAfterRebufferMs` 2 s → ~10 s so a recovered stall doesn't immediately re-stall, while explicitly **not** blindly enlarging the buffer (`heapgrowthlimit` is 192 MB against a 128 MB default video buffer; GC pressure is itself an untested stutter hypothesis); FR-R216-4 accumulates dropped frames / rebuffer count+duration / bandwidth estimate / decoder name / link state and fire-and-forgets them to Phase 177's QoE endpoint. Invariants: no user-visible setting ever (viewers never choose player, bitrate or quality), report-never-guess, telemetry is never a playback dependency. Out of scope: 24p matching (nothing to switch to on this panel), replacing ExoPlayer with libmpv/custom extractors (the supplied third-party DV-demux theory was checked and disproved — the DV path here is correct and hardware-accelerated), mid-playback re-negotiation, continuous link polling, and any change to `RaviloRenderers`' audio/video split. Built 2026-08-28 (compiles clean on Android + wasmJs); the link-permission open question resolved from Android platform docs (`ACCESS_WIFI_STATE`/`ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` are both normal, non-runtime permissions); not yet run on real hardware (stue TV / Pixel 9) — no confirmed on-device decode-ceiling, link-state or QoE values yet. | [spec](specs/ravilo/requirements/phase-R216-player-delivery-resilience.md) |
